20:["$","p",null,{"children":["Every operation is charged ",["$","code",null,{"children":"charged = int(flop_cost × dtype_rate × complex_factor × weight)"}],"."]}]
21:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Four factors, on purpose."}]," Each factor isolates one kind of reasoning so it can be\nread off and audited on its own:"]}]
22:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}]}]," — the operation count. Every shape- and algorithm-dependent term lives\nhere, so anyone — us or a participant — can read it off the formula and audit it\nfunction by function."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["$","code",null,{"children":"dtype_rate"}]}]," — the width factor. One billed FLOP is one 32-bit-class real\noperation; a 64-bit-class operand bills ",["$","code",null,{"children":"2×"}],". Like the weight ladder this is a ",["$","em",null,{"children":"policy"}],"\ntable (subject to tuning), not shape math — defined in\n",["$","$L16",null,{"href":"#dtype-and-precision","children":"Dtype and precision"}],"."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["$","code",null,{"children":"complex_factor"}]}]," — the complex-structure factor. It is ",["$","code",null,{"children":"1"}]," for real dtypes and, for\ncomplex dtypes, the number of real (component-precision) operations one billed unit\nexpands into (a complex multiply is 6 real FLOPs, a complex add 2). It is ",["$","em",null,{"children":"math"}],", not\npolicy — a textbook decomposition fixed per op, and computed per call for the\ncontraction family from the same accumulation that produces ",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}],"."]}],"\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":["$","code",null,{"children":"weight"}]}]," — the hardware tier. A separate per-element factor for how much more one\nelement costs on real hardware: a plain sequential add or write is ",["$","code",null,{"children":"1"}],", a\nnon-sequential memory access (a sort's comparison order, a computed-index gather, a\nrandom-reorder draw) is ",["$","code",null,{"children":"4"}],", and a transcendental element (",["$","code",null,{"children":"sin"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"exp"}],", …) does many\ntimes more floating-point work at ",["$","code",null,{"children":"16"}],". Rather than bill each op its exact measured\nratio — noisy, machine-specific, and hard to audit — we group operations into a small\nfixed ladder of tiers (",["$","code",null,{"children":"{0, 1, 4, 16}"}],"); that grouping is a deliberate\ncompetition-design choice."]}],"\n"]}]
23:["$","p",null,{"children":["For a real 32-bit workload ",["$","code",null,{"children":"dtype_rate"}]," and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"complex_factor"}]," are both ",["$","code",null,{"children":"1"}],", so the bill is\nexactly ",["$","code",null,{"children":"int(flop_cost × weight)"}]," and nothing here changes."]}]
24:["$","p",null,{"children":["The rule that keeps the split honest — and the model non-gameable — is one ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"separation\ninvariant"}],": ",["$","em",null,{"children":"a shape or algorithm constant never lives in a weight"}]," (anything depending\non a matrix dimension or loop length belongs in ",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}],"); ",["$","em",null,{"children":["width policy never lives in\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}]," or ",["$","code",null,{"children":"weight"}]]}]," (it lives in ",["$","code",null,{"children":"dtype_rate"}],"); and ",["$","em",null,{"children":["complex structure lives in\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"complex_factor"}]]}]," — computed per call for the contraction family from the same\ndecomposition that produces ",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}],". 25:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"We bill the textbook standard-algorithm cost, not literal BLAS/LAPACK."}],"\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"linalg.inv"}]," is billed ",["$","code",null,{"children":"2n³"}]," (the standard LU-based ",["$","code",null,{"children":"dgetrf"}],"+",["$","code",null,{"children":"dgetri"}]," operation\ncount) regardless of what the underlying library does; top-k SVD is billed as the\nstandard truncated-algorithm cost. 34:["$","h3",null,{"className":"flex scroll-m-28 flex-row items-center gap-2","id":"the-unifying-philosophy--every-byte-written-is-metered","children":[["$","a",null,{"data-card":"","href":"#the-unifying-philosophy--every-byte-written-is-metered","className":"peer","children":"The unifying philosophy — every byte written is metered"}],["$","$L15",null,{"iconNode":"$5:props:children:0:props:children:1:props:iconNode","className":"lucide-link size-3.5 shrink-0 text-fd-muted-foreground opacity-0 transition-opacity peer-hover:opacity-100","aria-hidden":true}]]}]
35:["$","blockquote",null,{"children":["\n",["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"flopscope meters both computation on values and the memory traffic that produces\nthem."}]," A ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"view"}]," is free — it creates no new buffer and touches no element. Any op\nthat ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"writes a new buffer"}]," is charged at least ",["$","code",null,{"children":"1"}]," per element written, whether the\nvalues it writes are computed (",["$","code",null,{"children":"sin(x)"}],"), copied (",["$","code",null,{"children":"concatenate"}],"), replicated\n(",["$","code",null,{"children":"tile"}],"), or a repeated constant (",["$","code",null,{"children":"ones"}],"). 36:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"The decision procedure"}]," — apply these steps in order to any op:"]}]
37:["$","ol",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"View / metadata only"}]," (returns a view or read-only reinterpretation of existing\nmemory, inspects shape/dtype, allocates no new buffer)? 4b:["$","p",null,{"children":"We identified two ways a dtype-blind meter under-counts:"}]
4c:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Complex packing."}]," A complex multiply does the work of four real multiplies and two\nreal adds, but a dtype-blind meter would bill ",["$","code",null,{"children":"x * z"}]," (with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"z"}]," complex) as one real\nmultiply. 4d:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Width (bit) packing."}]," A float64 lane is twice as wide as a float32 lane. 61:["$","p",null,{"children":["The billing dtype follows all work the call performs: the selected compute loop and any\ndestination buffer it materializes. Each op declares those participants at its deduct\nsite. Ordinary operands promote with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"np.result_type"}],"; Python scalars follow NumPy's\nNEP 50 weak promotion, so ",["$","code",null,{"children":"f32_array * 2.0"}]," stays float32 (the scalar does not up-promote\nthe array). Single-output binary pointwise ufuncs — and their ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ufunc.outer"}]," spellings —\nderive their compute price from NumPy's complete resolved loop signature, including every\narithmetic input slot and every output slot. That complete signature also determines\nwhether the registry's complex-arithmetic factor applies. The promoted operands provide an\ninput-rate floor: it can raise only ",["$","code",null,{"children":"dtype_rate"}],", and does not invent complex arithmetic\nwhen NumPy resolves a non-complex numeric loop (for example, a predicate's boolean\noutput). ",["$","code",null,{"children":"out="}]," then joins as a separate participant under the\nwidest-participating-buffer doctrine."]}]
62:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"A control operand is not an arithmetic operand."}]," One ufunc slot in NumPy's table names\na number that ",["$","em",null,{"children":"steers"}]," the arithmetic instead of being arithmetic, and it prices neither\nthe loop nor the floor. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ldexp(x1, x2)"}]," computes ",["$","code",null,{"children":"x1 * 2**x2"}],": ",["$","code",null,{"children":"x2"}]," is the exponent, and\nNumPy ships ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ei->e"}]," / ",["$","code",null,{"children":"fi->f"}]," / ",["$","code",null,{"children":"di->d"}]," (and their ",["$","code",null,{"children":"l"}]," variants) precisely so a wide\nexponent does not drag the mantissa up a precision tier. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ldexp(f32, int32_array)"}],"\nresolves ",["$","code",null,{"children":"fi->f"}],", computes ",["$","code",null,{"children":"float32"}],", and bills at the float32 rate (",["$","code",null,{"children":"1000"}]," for a\n1,000-element call) — identical to ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ldexp(f32, int8_array)"}],", because it is identical\nmantissa work producing identical values. 64:["$","ul",null,{"children":["\n",["$","li",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Mixed kinds promote up."}]," ",["$","code",null,{"children":"matmul(int32_array, float32_array)"}]," resolves to ",["$","code",null,{"children":"float64"}],"\n(numpy's promotion for that mix) and bills at the float64 rate — the same as a genuine\nfloat64 matmul. 66:["$","p",null,{"children":["A reduction is billed at the dtype numpy actually accumulates in. 67:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"reduceat"}],"'s base cost follows numpy's own per-segment semantics rather than a flat\nper-element charge: for segment ",["$","code",null,{"children":"i"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"indices[i] < indices[i+1]"}]," reduces the elements\nbetween them (a length-",["$","code",null,{"children":"L"}]," segment costs ",["$","code",null,{"children":"L-1"}]," applications, the same ",["$","code",null,{"children":"n-1"}]," convention\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"reduce"}]," uses), while a non-monotonic pair (",["$","code",null,{"children":"indices[i] >= indices[i+1]"}],") is a plain\nelement copy with no arithmetic — the segment costs 0. The final segment always runs to\nthe end of the axis. The exact call-level base formula is\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"max(lanes × applications_per_lane, lanes × produced_cells_per_lane, 1)"}],". The first two\nentries are the variable-work terms: actual ufunc applications across segments and cells\nproduced per lane. The cell term accounts for singleton/copy branches that emit without\ninvoking the ufunc; the final term retains a call-level minimum of one. The resulting\nbase cost is priced through the standard resolved accumulator dtype rate, complex\nfactor, and inherited base-ufunc weight. Ordinary long segments remain\narithmetic-dominated; a single whole-axis segment of 1000 elements bills 999\napplications. When the reduced axis has length one, the produced-cell term can make\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"reduceat(a, [0])"}]," bill more than ",["$","code",null,{"children":"reduce(a)"}],"; this is intentional because ",["$","code",null,{"children":"reduceat"}],"\nretains its per-produced-cell floor even when the segment performs no arithmetic.\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"ufunc.outer"}]," is not itself an accumulating reduction, but an explicit\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"dtype="}]," on it resolves through the same request-is-the-loop rule: the dtype names the\nloop numpy actually runs, not a discount, so\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"np.multiply.outer(int32_arr, int32_arr, dtype=float64)"}]," bills the float64 rate over the\nfull outer-product grid — double the default int32-loop total."]}]

The two untrusted ingress points, ","$L16e"," and a ","$L16f"," ","$L170",", therefore validate the claim (raising ","$L171"," on a mismatch, charging ","$L172"," for a genuine one, ","$L173"," = non-identity generators, ","$L174",") and then canonicalize: each orbit takes the value at its lexicographically smallest index, so values that survived only within the tolerance do not reach the tag. Data already exactly invariant is passed through unchanged, so the charge and the zero-copy view semantics are unaffected; the caller's array is never modified. Symmetry a flopscope op derives ","$L175"," (e.g. ","$L176"," propagating an already-validated operand's tag, or a slice/transpose view) is exempt, since it never carries a fresh caller-supplied claim. ","$L177"," this is a property of those two ingress points, not a package-wide invariant — a Reynolds projection sums each orbit in a fixed element order, so its own output is typically invariant only to about an ulp, as is a symmetric matmul. That residue is rounding rather than caller-placed information, so it is sound for accounting; code needing bit-exactness should use an ingress point or ","$L178",". In-process code can attach a tag regardless (","$L179"," plus an attribute assignment needs nothing from this package), so the boundary that holds is the wire: the server dispatches registered ops only, and none of those names is registered"]}],"$L17a"]}],"$L17b","$L17c","$L17d","$L17e","$L17f","$L180"]}]]}]}]

9c:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","strong",null,{"children":"Family rule"}],": ",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost = numel(output)"}]," for a single-output op. A ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"multi-output\nufunc"}]," (",["$","code",null,{"children":"divmod"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"frexp"}],", ",["$","code",null,{"children":"modf"}]," — the only NumPy ufuncs with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nout > 1"}]," flopscope\ncounts) is priced as ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nout"}]," independent applications of the reference algorithm, so its\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost = nout × numel(output)"}],": ",["$","code",null,{"children":"divmod"}]," bills what running ",["$","code",null,{"children":"floor_divide"}]," and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"mod"}],"\nseparately would bill, combined; ",["$","code",null,{"children":"modf"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"frexp"}]," bill twice a one-output unary of the same\nshape. The justification is ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"not"}]," the write-metering principle above — arithmetic\nweight, not buffer traffic, dominates these ops, so that clause does not govern them.\nThe justification is the model's standing rule that pricing tracks a ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"standardized\nreference algorithm"}],", not whatever compute-sharing a specific backend implementation\nhappens to do — stated earlier as \"we bill the textbook standard-algorithm cost, not\nliteral BLAS/LAPACK\" (see ",["$","$L16",null,{"href":"#billing-model--design-principles","children":"Billing model & design\nprinciples"}],") and already the basis for the FMA=2\nconvention and the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"trapz"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"nanstd"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"linspace"}]," fixes. A backend that computes several\noutputs more cheaply than the sum of independent calls does not earn its caller a\ndiscount; without this rule, an efficient combined op becomes a way to obtain two\ndeliverables for the price of one. Measured backend timing makes this explicit rather\nthan contradicting it: at N=500,000, ",["$","code",null,{"children":"np.divmod"}]," runs in ~0.98x a single\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"np.floor_divide"}]," (~0.49x ",["$","code",null,{"children":"floor_divide"}],"+",["$","code",null,{"children":"mod"}]," combined) — NumPy's ",["$","code",null,{"children":"divmod"}]," genuinely\nshares the division and derives the remainder almost for free — yet flopscope prices it\nat ",["$","code",null,{"children":"2×"}]," ",["$","code",null,{"children":"floor_divide"}]," anyway, by the reference-algorithm rule above; the bill is a\ndeliberate ceiling on what a participant could otherwise obtain via an efficient\ncombined op, not a measurement of ",["$","code",null,{"children":"divmod"}],"'s own backend cost. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"out="}]," does not multiply\nthis a second time — the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nout"}]," scaling lives in ",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}]," (the cell-count axis),\nwhich is independent of the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"out="}]," destination-dtype-rate axis described in ",["$","$L16",null,{"href":"#which-dtype-prices-a-call","children":"Which\ndtype prices a call"}],"; supplying ",["$","code",null,{"children":"out="}]," widens the rate\nexactly as it would for any other op, never the cell count."]}]

a7:["$","p",null,{"children":["Ops that do more than one accumulation pass carry the extra passes in\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}]," (never in the weight column): the variance family makes four\npasses (mean-sum, centre, square, variance-sum), ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ptp"}]," makes two (max + min)\nplus the per-output subtract, and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"mean"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"average"}]," add the per-output divide.\nA ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nan*"}]," variant adds a further ",["$","code",null,{"children":"numel(input)"}]," on top of its plain sibling's\nformula, for the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"isnan"}]," test pass the ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nan*"}]," form runs before reducing that\nits plain sibling does not — ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"but only where NumPy actually runs that pass"}],".\nNine of the factory-built ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nan*"}]," reductions go through NumPy's\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"_replace_nan"}],", which returns no mask for a non-inexact dtype, so an\n",["$","strong",null,{"children":["integer or bool input runs no ",["$","code",null,{"children":"isnan"}]," pass and bills exactly like its plain\nsibling"]}],"; only float/complex input carries the surcharge. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nanmedian"}],",\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"nanpercentile"}],", and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nanquantile"}]," instead go through ",["$","code",null,{"children":"_remove_nan_1d"}],", which\ncalls ",["$","code",null,{"children":"np.isnan"}]," whatever the dtype, so those three carry it for every dtype.\n",["$","strong",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"nanmax"}]," and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nanmin"}]," carry it at no dtype at all"]}],": for any plain\nnon-object ndarray — which is every operand flopscope hands them — NumPy\ntakes a fast path that reduces with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"fmax"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"fmin"}]," and then tests the ",["$","em",null,{"children":"reduced\noutput"}],", never the input, so there is no input-sized pass to charge and both\nbill exactly like ",["$","code",null,{"children":"max"}],"/",["$","code",null,{"children":"min"}],". Like every other\npass in these ops, the surcharge is ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"orbit-mapped"}],": on a symmetric operand it\ncounts unique elements, not dense ",["$","code",null,{"children":"numel"}],". The pass is folded into the same\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"flop_cost"}]," as the rest of the reduction, so it is charged as one additional\npass over the input priced at the op's own rate/factor — not a separate flat\nrate: it inherits the host op's complex factor (e.g. 6.0 on ",["$","code",null,{"children":"nanprod"}],") and\nits resolved accumulator dtype (e.g. the widened int64 rate on an int32\ninput), the same way the variance family and ",["$","code",null,{"children":"ptp"}]," already blend multiple\npasses under one op-level factor."]}]

b4:["$","p",null,{"children":[["$","code",null,{"children":"einsum"}]," runs the accumulation directly as ",["$","code",null,{"children":"(K − 1)·M + α"}],", where ",["$","code",null,{"children":"α"}]," is the\nnumber of unique (output + contracted) index combinations — equal to ",["$","code",null,{"children":"K·M"}]," for a\nsingle clean contraction, but more general for multi-index or broadcast\nsubscripts. Batched/stacked variants of any row\nabove multiply the closed form by the batch size. The path search that picks\nthis binary order runs unbilled (see the ",["$","strong",null,{"children":"No free path-search wall time"}],"\ninvariant in ",["$","$L16",null,{"href":"#non-exploitability","children":"§Non-exploitability"}],"), so at ",["$","code",null,{"children":"k ≥ 8"}],"\noperands only an allowlisted, linear-time ",["$","code",null,{"children":"optimize="}]," choice runs verbatim —\nevery other choice, including every ",["$","code",null,{"children":"opt_einsum"}]," alias for an exhaustive or\nsuperlinear search, is downgraded to ",["$","code",null,{"children":"'greedy'"}]," before the search runs."]}]

121:["$","p",null,{"children":["For ",["$","code",null,{"children":"pad"}]," stat modes: numpy pads axes in order ",["$","code",null,{"children":"0..ndim-1"}],", mutating one shared output\nbuffer in place, so axis ",["$","code",null,{"children":"i"}],"'s reduction reads a cross-section built from axes ",["$","code",null,{"children":"< i"}]," at\ntheir ",["$","em",null,{"children":"already-padded"}]," size and axes ",["$","code",null,{"children":"> i"}]," at their original size:\n",["$","code",null,{"children":"cross_i = ∏_{j<i} grown_j · ∏_{j>i} in_shape[j]"}],", where ",["$","code",null,{"children":"grown_j = in_shape[j] + before_j + after_j"}]," is axis ",["$","code",null,{"children":"j"}],"'s final padded length. ",["$","code",null,{"children":"stat_len_i = min(stat_length_i, in_shape[i])"}]," (default = full axis).
